115 年 9 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
9 月出口 872.2 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.9%，較上年
同月增 60.9%(按新臺幣計算增 68.2%)；第3 季出口 2,449.2 億美元，
較上年同季增 44.6%；累計 1 至 9 月 6,615.5 億美元，較上年同期增
46.2%(按新臺幣計算增 49.1%)。 
（二）進口 
9 月進口 635.9 億美元，亦創歷年單月新高，較上月增 5.8%，較上
年同月增 51.7%(按新臺幣計算增 58.6%)；第 3 季進口 1,818.0 億美
元，較上年同季增 44.3%；累計 1 至 9 月 5,010.0 億美元，較上年同
期增 41.3%(按新臺幣計算增 43.8%)。 
（三）出入超 
9 月出超 236.3 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 113.4 億美元；
累計 1 至 9 月出超 1,605.5 億美元，較上年同期增 625.4 億美元。

二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於創新科技應用商機持續擴展，推升相關供應鏈出貨動能，加上
消費性電子新品備貨需求與產品價格上漲效應之挹注， 9 月出口較
上年同月增 330.0 億美元(+60.9%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 資通與視聽產品出口 391.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增
198.6 億美元(+1.0 倍)， 其中電腦及其附屬單元增157.4 億美元(+1.1
倍)、儲存媒體增 17.6 億美元(+2.2 倍)、交換器及路由器增 10.5
億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
2. 電子零組件出口 311.3 億美元(歷年單月次高)， 較上年同月增97.1
億美元(+45.3%)，其中積體電路增 93.9 億美元(+46.1%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+33.7%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.8 億美元(+32.0%)。 
3. 礦產品出口 22.6 億美元，較上年同月增 10.5 億美元(+86.4%)，其
中石油煉製品增 10.1 億美元(+99.0%)。 
4. 機械出口 27.4 億美元(歷年單月次高)，較上年同月增 6.4 億美元
(+30.7%)，其中生產半導體等機械增 2.7 億美元(+61.0%)、未列名
具特殊功能機械增 0.6 億美元(+73.6%)。 
5. 基本金屬及其製品出口 26.9 億美元，較上年同月增 5.0 億美元
(+22.9%)，其中銅及其製品增 2.5 億美元(+65.1%)、鋼鐵及其製品
增 1.4 億美元(+11.7%)。

1. 第 3 季出口較上年同季增 755.9 億美元(+44.6%)，以資通與視聽產
品增 367.6 億美元(+55.0%)、電子零組件增 308.4 億美元(+51.2%)
增加較多。 
2. 累計 1 至 9 月出口較上年同期增 2,089.8 億美元(+46.2%)，其中資
通與視聽產品增 1,222.2 億美元(+72.4%)、電子零組件增 726.6 億
美元(+45.7%)。

等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
隨 AI 產業鏈全球分工與出口引申需求擴增，電子零組件、資通產品
及半導體設備進口維持強勁，加上國際農工原料行情處於高檔，9
月進口較上年同月增 216.7 億美元(+51.7%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口440.5 億美元， 較上年同月增163.9億美元(+59.3%)，
其中電子零組件增 112.6 億美元(+82.5%)、電腦之零附件增 19.4
億美元(+1.5 倍)、礦產品增 17.1 億美元(+38.9%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 128.0 億美元， 較上年同月增 36.0 億美元(+39.1%)，
其中資通設備增 18.4 億美元(+75.0%)、半導體設備增 12.4 億美元
(+34.9%)，交通運輸設備減 1.4 億美元(-46.4%)。 
3. 消費品進口 62.1 億美元，較上年同月增 16.5 億美元(+36.0%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增 16.4 億美元(+5.3 倍)、筆電增 0.5 億
美元(+43.8%)，藥粧用品減 1.2 億美元(-16.6%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月對東協出口創新高，對美國、陸港為單月次高 
1. 對美國出口 274.6 億美元，較上年同月增 141.5 億美元(+1.1 倍)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 124.1 億美元(+1.3 倍)、 電子零組件增10.1
億美元(+1.3 倍)、機械增 2.2 億美元(+42.3%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 206.2 億美元，較上年同月增 47.8 億美元
(+30.2%)，其中電子零組件增 34.9 億美元(+30.5%)、資通與視聽
產品增 5.7 億美元(+39.0%)、機械增 1.9 億美元(+43.7%)。 
3. 對東協出口 188.8 億美元，較上年同月增 91.2 億美元(+93.4%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 49.7 億美元(+2.2 倍)、電子零組件增 32.0
億美元(+63.3%)、礦產品增 4.8 億美元(+1.4 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 45.3 億美元，較上年同月增 7.7 億美元(+20.6%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 5.7 億美元(+34.1%)、電子零組件增 2.2 億美
元(+56.6%)，基本金屬及其製品減 0.8 億美元(-20.5%)。 
5. 對日本出口 30.7 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+13.4%)，其
中電子零組件增 1.5 億美元(+11.0%)、基本金屬及其製品增 0.6 億
美元(+32.5%)、機械增 0.5 億美元(+29.1%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月對美國出口 2,077.8 億美元，較上年同期增 773.1 億美
元(+59.3%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,600.9 億美元，增 355.3 億美
元(+28.5%)， 對東協出口1,339.6 億美元 ， 增463.6 億美元(+52.9%)，
對歐洲出口 421.8 億美元 ， 增138.4 億美元(+48.8%)， 對日本出口277.7
億美元，增 58.3 億美元(+26.6%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
9 月自中國大陸與香港進口 135.0 億美元 ， 較上年同月增50.1 億美元
(+59.1%)，自南韓進口 116.3 億美元，增52.6 億美元(+82.5%)，自東
協進口 106.0 億美元，增 52.6 億美元(+98.4%)，自日本進口 64.3 億
美元，增 17.7 億美元(+38.0%)，自歐洲進口 50.2 億美元，增 3.5 億
美元(+7.4%)，自美國進口 48.1 億美元，增 6.5 億美元(+15.5%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 9 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增77.6%、
自南韓增 65.8%、自中國大陸與香港增 42.8%、自日本增 25.7%、自
美國增 21.4%、自歐洲增 10.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）主要國家/地區今年 1-8 月外貿景氣普遍走升，以南韓(1-9 月)出口年
增 56.8%最為強勁，香港、新加坡亦分別年增 49.0%、39.8%；中國
大陸、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-7 月)、日本各增
9.7%、8.3%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-8 月以香港進口年增 50.5%較
突出，新加坡、中國大陸分別年增 37.3%、27.0%，南韓(1-9 月)、德
國(1-7 月)分別增 19.7%、10.0%，日本、美國各增 7.8%、4.2%。

### Page 7

7
圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）受惠於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及雲端服務等應用商機持續擴展，
加以步入傳統外銷旺季 、產品價格上漲效應，9 月出口 872.2 億美元，
年增 60.9%，連續 35 個月正成長；進口635.9 億美元，年增51.7%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口引申需求擴張，及國際原物料行情居
高；出、進口值及出超 236.3 億美元齊創單月新高。累計 1 至 9 月出
口年增 46.2%、進口增 41.3%。
（二）9 月主要貨品出口普遍較上年同月成長，以資通與視聽產品增 1.0 倍
最佳，包括顯示卡、伺服器、儲存媒體、 交換器及路由器等出口均
呈倍數增加 ；其次為礦產品增 86.4%，主因全球油品供應偏緊支撐價
格維持高檔；電子零組件隨 AI 應用及消費性電子新品備貨需求升溫，
增 45.3%，機械、電機產品亦各增 3 成上下。累計 1 至 9 月資通與視
聽產品、電子零組件出口併計增 59.5%，占總出口比重 79.0%。

（三）主要市場方面，9 月對美國、東協出口值分創歷年單月次高、新高，
年增率各為 1.1 倍及 93.4%，合計對總出口增加貢獻達 7 成；對中國
大陸與香港、歐洲、日本出口各年增 30.2%、20.6%、13.4%。累計 1
至 9 月對五大市場出口值皆為歷年同期最高，其中對美國、東協均
提前刷新全年紀錄 ， 分別年增59.3%、52.9%，對歐洲出口年增48.8%，
對陸港、日本各增 28.5%、26.6%。
（四）展望未來，全球貿易政策及地緣政治情勢變化等，仍增添總體 經濟
不確定性，惟隨人工智慧應用 持續深化，國際雲端服務業者 及各國
加速 AI 基礎建設投資，帶動半導體、伺服器等相關供應鏈需求擴增，
加以科技新品備貨、中國大陸及歐美年底採購旺季將屆， 均有利挹
注我國外銷動能，第 4 季出口可望延續成長態勢。

AI 資金壓力拖累科技股，惟產業需求仍具韌性
2026 年 10 月 23 日
一、 市場動態
1. 費半再度重挫，科技股成為美股主要賣壓來源：美國股市主要指數 10 月 22 日跌多漲
少， 道瓊工業指數上漲0.02%，收52,867.98 點；S&P 500 指數下跌 1.21%，收7,663.43
點；Nasdaq 綜合指數下跌 2.60%，收 26,008.86 點，創 6 月 5 日以來最大單日跌幅
。費城半導體指數重挫 6.72%，收 10,731.98 點，跌幅明顯大於主要股價指數，顯示
AI 及半導體族群仍是本波市場修正的主要壓力來源。

2. AI 相關企業全面承壓 ， 資金流向非科技股及公債 ：10 月 22 日 Meta 下跌約 7%、Lam
Research 跌幅逾一成，Nvidia 下跌 4.4%，其他 AI 相關股票亦明顯走低，顯示市場
對 AI 資本支出及投資報酬的疑慮已由大型科技平台延伸至半導體與基礎建設供應商
。不過，道瓊上漲 0.02%，羅素 2000 指數亦小幅收紅，反映資金並未全面撤出股市
；美國 10 年期公債殖利率則因避險需求跌破 5%，顯示資金由高評價科技股轉向其他
產業及避險資產。
3. 中東地緣政治風險升溫，油價攀至 5 月以來高點：中東能源運輸及供給風險 10 月 22
日再度升高，布蘭特原油價格上漲約 3.76%，升至每桶 113 美元附近，創 5 月以來高
點。油價攀升除使市場重新關注能源成本對通膨與 Fed 政策路徑的影響，也可能增加
AI 資料中心的電力及營運成本。中東情勢雖非本次半導體股重挫的唯一因素，但在市
場已擔憂 AI 投資效益的情況下，能源價格上揚進一步壓抑投資人風險偏好。

二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 殖利率回落難抵科技股下跌，顯示修正因素出現轉變：10 月 14 日費半重挫主要受到
美國 9 月 CPI 高於預期、升息疑慮及美債殖利率盤中急升影響，惟當日 10 年期殖利
率收盤反而小幅下降；10 月 22 日情況則更加明顯，美國 10 年期公債殖利率因避險
需求盤中跌破 5%，費半仍大跌 6.72%。這顯示市場關注焦點已由單純的利率與估值
壓力，逐步轉向 AI 資本支出的資金來源、融資能力及投資回收期。因此，即使後續殖
利率回落，若企業仍無法證明AI 投資能轉化為足夠營收與現金流，科技股評價仍可能
持續受到壓抑。
2. Meta 自由現金流轉負 ，AI 投資成本由預期轉為實際財務壓力 ：Meta 第三季營收 646.0
億美元、年增 26%，顯示核心業務仍維持強勁成長；但資本支出達 349.1 億美元，超
過同期營業現金流 327.9 億美元，使自由現金流由去年同期正 106.3 億美元轉為負
21.2 億美元。公司亦將全年資本支出預估調整至 1,350～1,450 億美元，並宣布設立

無表決權 Class C 股票，增加未來資本運用彈性。這反映 AI 投資規模已開始超越部分

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
大型科技企業當期現金創造能力，但 Meta 仍持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有
價證券，不能將單季自由現金流轉負直接解讀為財務危機。
3. 資本稀缺逐漸成為 AI 擴張瓶頸，新興雲端業者面臨更大壓力：AI 發展需要持續投入
GPU、伺服器、資料中心及電力基礎建設，龐大資金需求使融資能力逐漸成為決定企
業成長速度的重要因素。相較具備穩定營業現金流與龐大資產規模的大型科技平台，
新興 AI 雲端服務商如 CoreWeave 及 Nebius 高度依賴外部融資，因而更容易受到借
貸成本、信用利差及投資人風險偏好變化影響。我們認為，這次修正並不代表 AI 運算

需求突然消失，而是市場開始重新評估不同企業能否負擔下一階段的擴張成本。
4. 半導體設備財報強勁卻遭賣壓，市場提高獲利兌現標準：Lam Research 於 10 月 21
日公布截至 2026 年 9 月季度（FY2027 Q1）營收 82.19 億美元，年增 54.4%、季增
22%，非 GAAP 營業利益率達 40.2%，下一季營收指引為 86 億美元、上下浮動 4 億
美元。然而，即使營運仍快速成長，10 月 22 日股價仍大幅下跌。我們研判，市場已
由過去重視 AI 訂單與營收成長，轉向同時檢視未來成長能否超越原先偏高預期，以及
AI 投資是否具備足夠的財務報酬。當股價已反映高度成長期待，即使財報表現良好，
也可能因評價調整與籌碼降溫而承壓。
5. 油價再度上漲，Fed 政策風險仍可能限制科技股評價：儘管 10 月 22 日美債殖利率因
避險需求下滑，但能源價格上漲仍可能延緩通膨降溫，使 Fed 後續政策路徑維持不確
定性。若油價持續高檔，不僅可能推升企業運輸、電力及營運成本，也將增加通膨預

期，進而限制長天期美債殖利率的下行空間。因此，科技股短線同時面臨AI 投資報酬
受到重新檢視 ， 以及能源價格與利率政策不確定的雙重壓力 。 後續10 月 28 日 FOMC
會後聲明及主席記者會，將是評估利率環境的重要依據。
三、 市場看法：
1. 股市下跌但多數個股上漲 ， 顯示賣壓集中於大型科技股 ：10 月 22 日 S&P 500 指數下
跌 1.21%，但根據美聯社（AP）報導，約三分之二成分股逆勢上漲，追蹤 S&P 500
等權重指數的 ETF（RSP）更上漲 0.73%，道瓊工業指數亦小幅收紅，呈現市值加權
指數與市場廣度明顯背離的現象 。這反映本波修正主要集中於權重較高的 AI 及大型科
技股，而非整體美股同步走弱。相較上週以防禦性類股輪動為主的情況，本週更值得
注意的是市場對高資本支出及高評價科技企業的重新定價，資金仍持續流向其他產業
，而非全面性風險撤離。只要賣壓未進一步擴散，現階段仍較接近科技股評價調整與
類股輪動，而非整體美股多頭格局反轉。
2. 台積電財測再度上修，顯示 AI 需求尚未出現結構性反轉：台積電 10 月 15 日法說會

將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先略高於 40%上修至約 44%， 第三季營收達新台幣
1.49 兆元、年增 50.9%，稅後純益年增 68.7%，每股盈餘 29.43 元、同樣年增 68.7%
；第四季美元營收預估為 523～543 億美元，以中間值計算季增約 14%。此外，Lam
Research 截至 9 月季度營收年增 54.4%，亦顯示半導體設備需求仍強勁。前述這些

### Page 3

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決

大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
數據與近期科技股重挫形成明顯對比 ， 反映目前市場主要擔憂AI 投資回收及股票評價
，而非半導體終端需求同步惡化。只要大型雲端服務商未全面縮減AI 資本支出，先進
製程與設備需求中期仍具支撐。
3. AI 投資進入報酬驗證階段，營收成長能否追上資本投入成為關鍵：我們認為，AI 產業
目前正由過去的資本支出擴張階段，逐步轉向投資報酬率與自由現金流的驗證階段。
以 Meta 為例，第三季營收仍成長 26%，但資本支出已超過營業現金流，顯示即使
AI 技術有助於改善廣告推薦及營收變現，龐大基礎建設投入仍可能使短期自由現金流

承壓。不過，資料中心及運算設備通常具有多年使用年限，不能僅以單季現金流評估
整體投資成敗。真正關鍵在於未來 AI 服務收入、營業利益及現金流成長，能否逐步抵
銷前期資本投入。具備明確商業化模式、較高資本效率及穩定現金流的企業，後續表
現可望優於高度依賴融資與成長預期的業者。
4. AI 融資風險仍集中於部分企業 ， 尚不足以認定信用危機全面擴散 ：Meta Q3 財報顯示
，該公司持有 824.5 億美元現金、約當現金及有價證券，顯示其具備一定財務緩衝能
力；相較之下，新興AI 雲端業者因資料中心建置成本龐大、現金流尚未成熟，對信用
市場與外部融資條件的敏感度相對較高 。雖然 AI 資本支出引發投資人重新評估企業信
用風險，但我們認為目前仍缺乏足夠證據認定風險已擴散至整體投資級債市場或銀行
體系。然而，後續仍宜密切觀察 AI 相關企業 CDS 與公司債利差變化、再融資能力及
主要金融機構授信條件對於科技類股及整體股債市場的後續影響。
5. 股價修正不代表企業獲利同步下修，財報表現仍有機會支撐科技股：儘管近期半導體

及 AI 相關股價回落，但台積電與Lam Research 最新財報顯示，部分供應鏈企業營收
及獲利仍維持高度成長，反映股票價格與企業基本面短期可能出現明顯落差。當市場
因融資及資本支出疑慮而降低評價倍數時，即使企業獲利持續增加，股價也可能暫時
承壓。然而，若後續大型科技公司公布的雲端營收、AI 訂單與獲利展望持續優於預期
，企業 EPS 上修仍有機會抵銷部分本益比壓縮壓力。因此，目前尚不宜將科技股價格
修正直接等同於 AI 產業景氣反轉，企業獲利能否持續兌現，仍是中期市場方向的重要
支撐。

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
6. Fed 與大型科技股財報進入驗證期，短線震盪可能延續：Fed 將於 10 月 27～28 日舉
行利率決策會議，市場除關注是否調整政策利率外，更應重視會後聲明及主席記者會
對通膨、油價與後續利率路徑的態度。另一方面，大型雲端服務商後續公布的財報，
將提供 AI 資本支出、雲端營收及投資效率的最新證據。短線科技股仍可能受到市場評
價偏高、融資風險及地緣政治因素影響而維持震盪；不過，只要企業獲利成長與 AI
需求未出現結構性反轉，中期科技產業基本面仍具支撐，現階段維持短線審慎、中期
偏正向的看法。
四、市場展望：

1. 科技股短線宜審慎觀察 ， 中期仍看好具基本面支撐的AI 產業 ：近期科技股受到 AI 資本支
出、融資能力與評價調整等因素影響，短線波動可能延續，尤其高度依賴外部資金及獲利
尚未成熟的 AI 基礎建設業者，仍面臨較大不確定性。然而，台積電及 Lam Research 最
新財報顯示，先進製程與半導體設備需求尚未出現全面轉弱。我們認為，科技股中期展望
仍偏正向，但市場將更重視企業營收成長、訂單能見度、自由現金流及資本效率。相較缺
乏穩定獲利的 AI 概念企業，具技術優勢、財務結構穩健及獲利成長能見度的科技企業，
風險報酬相對有利。
2. AI 產業由全面成長轉向基本面分化，現金流將成為選股關鍵：隨著大型科技企業持續擴
大 AI 基礎建設投資，市場對未來投資報酬率的要求將同步提高。就目前已公布的科技股
財報結果來看，AI 產業長期成長趨勢尚未改變，但後續股價表現將更加分化，具備穩定
現金流、明確訂單及商業化能力的企業，相對有利；反之，對於高度依賴外部融資、自由
現金流持續為負且缺乏明確獲利時程的企業，仍宜維持審慎。

3. 美債避險功能重現，中天期債券風險報酬相對均衡：半導體類股 10/22 重挫，美國公債
價格上揚、10 年期殖利率盤中跌破 5%，顯示股市風險偏好下降時，美債仍可提供一定
避險效果。雖然 Fed 政策路徑及能源價格仍存在不確定性，但預期相較受政策利率影響
較大的短天期公債，以及受財政赤字、國債供給與期限溢價影響的超長天期公債，中天期
美國公債的風險報酬仍相對較為均衡 。 投資級債因企業信用基本面整體仍具韌性 ， 中期維
持偏正向看法；高收益債則須留意 AI 相關發行人信用風險上升及利差擴大的影響。

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對義隆電的投資建議維持中立 ， 係因(1)受到產業影響 ，3Q26
財報大幅不如預期 ，(2)4Q26 淡季更淡 ， 預估下修幅度不小 ，(3)27
年產業狀況仍受到 CPU、記憶體等元件影響，目前較難樂觀看待，
(4)下修 26、27 年獲利預估。綜合以上，下修義隆電 26 年 EPS 預
估為 6.26 元(原估 9.37 元，-22%YOY)，及預估 27 年 EPS 為 6.58
元(原估 10.14 元，+5.1%YOY)，因為 NB 市場除了先前的記憶體影
響外 ， 目前更受到CPU 缺貨的影響 ， 短線上義隆電的營運動能受到
連帶影響大 ， 靜待無人機相關IC 營收在 2H27 放大 ， 故投資建議維
持中立，目標價 132 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅滑落，獲利不如預期： 
義隆電 3Q26 營收僅為 22.2 億元(原估 30.85 億元)，衰退
30.7%QOQ，衰退33%YOY，營收大規模衰退，除了記憶體漲
價外，因為Agent AI 興起，使得CPU 缺貨嚴重，另外因為
成本反映至終端產品，故使得需求更是急凍。毛利率僅
44.7%，相對於2Q26 下滑 2.7 個百分點，主要就是各類成
本漲價，包含晶圓代工、封裝及 TOUCH PAD 內的 PCB 及被
動元件等，另外產品組合也轉差所致。另外業外又有高達
1 億元的金融評價損失，故使得稅後淨利僅為 1.68 億元
(原估 6.23 億元)，衰退 80.9%QOQ，衰退 79.1%YOY，EPS 
0.55 元(原估 EPS 2.05 元)。3Q26 產品組合為：AI 14%，
觸控 73%， 非觸控13%， 其中TOUCH PAD 43%，TOUCH SCREEN 
14%，指紋 16%，POINT STICK 19%，MCU+SENSOR 4%。

2. 3Q26 未給展望，但受到 CPU 缺貨影響嚴重： 
義隆電因為最近四季合併（個別）財務報表營業外收支淨
額合計數占稅前損益合計數之絕對值若大於 10%，在法說
會中不能提及預測性資訊，但在法說會有提到 4Q26 本來
就是季節性淡季，另外客戶在上半年非常積極拉貨，下半
年開始調整庫存，故 4Q26 推估各產線仍有下滑，僅有 AI
相關產品會成長。故研究部下修 4Q26 營收預估為 16.5 億
元(原估 31.7 億元)，衰退25.7%QOQ，衰退42.3%YOY，稅
後淨利同步下修預估為 1.51 億元(原估 6.38 億元)，衰退
10.1%QOQ， 衰退79.5%YOY，EPS 0.5 元(原估 EPS 2.1 元)。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 對 27 年 NB 產業看法仍未定，但義隆仍有新商機： 
義隆電在法說會表示，NB 產業有各研調機構都有看法，目
前普遍較為保守，但未來仍端視 27 年記憶體、各元件及
CPU 的狀況，非單一供應商決定市況，故認為在此階段仍
不易下結論。但義隆電在 NB 觸控相關 IC 方面，因為美系
競爭對手被併購後 ， 義隆電有SHARE GAIN 部分市場 ， 另外
也提供更廣泛的產品選項供客戶選擇，故公司認為本身的
NB 相關 IC 出貨量 27 年將優於市場表現。

4. 無人機貢獻性確實提升，大貢獻性將落在 2H27： 
1H26 無人機營收占比約低個位數，3Q26 已經達到 14%，
4Q26 有望在持續攀升。目前主要配合的客戶為國內廠商，
該國內廠商前天六項標案全數得標，而義隆電現在主要出
貨的產品為 CAMERA 模組，目前為兩個鏡頭一個模組 +AI 
BOX，單價為數十美元，目前送樣 4 鏡頭+避障+AI BOX 的
解決方案，單價高達 800 美元，有望在 1H27 逐步開始量
產，下半年完整貢獻。推估 AI 產品營收在 27 年成長約
30%YOY 以上。

5. 大幅下修26年預估，27年預估也較為保守：  
3Q26受到客戶已經提前拉貨影響，加上相關零組件及封裝
成本提升，使得3Q26營收及獲利大幅不如預期，加上4Q26
又有下修，故研究部下修26年義隆電營收預估為103.02億
元(原估126.8億元)，EPS下修預估至6.26元(原估EPS 9.37
元)。 展望27年 ， 目前研究部以27年NB整體出貨量高雙位數
衰退，但義隆電整體為低雙位數衰退，雖然義隆電無人機
相關產品推進至整合型產品，單價大幅提升下，仍下修27
年營收預估為112.3億元(原估140億元)， 成長9%YOY， 稅後
淨利下修預估為20億元(原估30.8億元)，成長5.1%YOY，
EPS 6.58元(原估10.14元)。

### Page 1

EQUITY: FABLESS
MediaTek   2454.TW 2454 TT
Global Markets Research
30 October 2026
Dispels recent market noises with upside surprise; retain Buy
Quick Note
Maintain Buy and TP of TWD5,800
We recently noted that MediaTek’s earnings call could prove to be a positive catalyst for 
its share price, considering a number of unfounded concerns and noises had taken center 
stage before the call (Preview: market concerns overdone ). However, we believe some of 
MTK’s messages during earnings call turned out to be even more bullish than we had 
expected, including: (1) TPU v.8 contribution of USD2.5bn in 4Q26E (vs. previous 
guidance of more than USD2bn , and Street concerns around MTK potentially missing the 
USD2bn target); (2) management now expects ASIC sales in 2027E to reach the high end 
(USD16bn) of its prior guidance range (USD14-16bn) vs. our forecast of USD14bn; (3) 
TPU v.9 schedule is on track for 1Q28E mass production (a powerful clarification vs. 
Street expectation of 1-2 quarters’ delay); (4) management is very confident about v.9 
EMIB-T future yield trend after resolving 2-3 key issues (implying no CoWoS back-up plan, 
vs. Street expectation of a CoWoS-based v.9 project being opened); (5) Mediatek 
announced that it has secured a 2nd ASIC customer with sales contribution from 2028E; 
(6) ASIC-driven operating leverage will kick in from 4Q26E (guidance suggesting 19% 
OPM in 4Q26E, vs our estimate of 18.6% and 3Q26’s 16%; and management expects 
2027E OPM to be above 20%, vs our estimate of 22%). The only downside surprise might 
be its 4Q26E sales guidance, which suggests 25%-32% q-q growth vs. our estimate of 
34% q-q. We would watch if this is a low bar for MediaTek to beat. GPM guidance of 
45%+-1.5% is 1pp lower than previous quarter’s range (46%+-1.5%; and 46.3% actual in 
3Q26) due to ASIC — in line with our forecast of 45.4%. Overall, we expect the share 
price to react positively to the earnings call and, thus, reiterate our Buy rating and 
TWD5,800 TP, based on 25x unchanged 2027-28 EPS estimate. The stock is currently 
trading at 16.9x.

Fig. 1: MediaTek's 3Q26 results and 4Q26F guidance vs Nomura forecasts and Bloomberg consensus

### Page 2

MediaTek: 3Q26 analysts briefing summary
Below comments are from MediaTek’s management, unless otherwise stated :
Results summary
3Q26 revenue was TWD168.1bn (+10.4% q-q, +18.3% y-y), above the high end of
the TWD152.2–159.8bn guidance.
The beat came mainly from better-than-expected customer demand and
improving supply.
○
Growth was driven by volume production of several new Smart Edge projects and
higher DRAM content in TV SoC.
○
•
GM was 46.3% (+0.1pp q-q, -0.2pp y-y), within the 46% ±1.5pp guidance.•

Opex ratio was 30.2% (vs 31.2% in 2Q26 and 30.9% in 3Q25), within the 31% ±2pp
guidance.
•
OPM was 16.1% (+1.1pp q-q, +0.5pp y-y).•
Net income was TWD28.2bn (+14.6% q-q, +10.8% y-y). Net margin was 16.8%
(+0.6pp q-q, -1.1pp y-y).
•
EPS was TWD17.50 (TWD15.28 in 2Q26, TWD15.84 in 3Q25). Non-TIFRS EPS was
TWD17.92.
•
Average FX was TWD31.99/USD (2Q26: 31.60; guidance assumption: 32).•
Prepared remarks
MediaTek completed a USD3.9bn overseas convertible bond offering in 3Q, with

participation from nVidia (NVDA US, Not rated) and Alphabet (GOOGL US, Not
rated).
This strengthens partnerships with key AI infrastructure players and helps secure
capacity for critical components.
○
•
MediaTek continues to make solid progress in both data center and edge devices.
Datacenter ASIC
Current gen: Demand remains very strong. Initial shipments began in
September, and MediaTek is confident of delivering USD2.5bn of revenue in
4Q26.
The 4Q upside comes from robust customer demand. MediaTek has

worked closely with supply chain partners to secure supply, including
substrates.
■
For 2027E, the program is expected to reach the high end of the revenue
target range, about USD16bn, or around 20% of the AI accelerator ASIC
market. This is underpinned by a solid supply outlook, and MediaTek is
working to secure additional capacity to meet demand.
■
•
Next gen: Development is progressing well and is on track for HVM in 1Q28.
The chip is purpose-built for the customer’s next-generation AI

infrastructure. It delivers multiple times higher performance per watt at a
significantly lower TCO.
■
It is expected to strengthen MediaTek’s market position and contribute
substantially to revenue growth.
■
•
Technology: Solid progress continues in 448G SerDes, 3.5D advanced
packaging, and CPO.
•
Broader customer base: MediaTek is expanding beyond silicon into system
and rack solutions. It connects custom xPUs to the NVLink Fusion ecosystem
and also supports UALink.

•

4Q26 guidance

becomes accretive from 4Q.

single-digit growth in USD.

Q&A
Any changes in ASIC schedule and milestones?
The 2nd ASIC is on track and on schedule.
MediaTek is aware of market noise about the tape-out schedule. The chip has

multiple dies, and a couple have already taped out.
○
The large, more complex die will also tape out on schedule, though it takes about
three weeks longer than the others.
○
•
2028 will be another year of strong y-y growth based on the demand profile. No
details yet, as the supply chain is still being finalized.
•
EMIB-T confidence
MediaTek is working with the US IDM’s packaging process and has built a strong in-
house manufacturing team led by experienced fab veterans.

•
It is working closely with the IDM and substrate suppliers.•
Management has high confidence that yield will improve quickly once two or three key
problems are resolved.
MediaTek is working with both Taiwanese and Japanese suppliers.○
•
MediaTek is not dependent on a single supplier.
TSMC (2330 TT, Buy) remains its most important partner, and the two work
closely across the entire technology chain.
○
•
Next-next gen, TAM and contribution
MediaTek is working very closely with the customer on the next-next generation.•

The customer is aggressive in pushing performance up and TCO down.•
This will be another challenging chip, and the definition will take time to finalize.•
Management will share more when the time is right.•

### Page 4

New ASIC client
A 2nd customer is confirmed, separate from the lead customer.•
The project has kicked off, with first revenue and mass production expected in 2028.•
The customer is not from China.•
Vendor financing for LLM customers?
MediaTek will consider it if necessary and if it is the right customer.•
Returns versus capex have improved over the past six quarters. Management 
believes capex, whether from CSPs or frontier model companies, will generate 
enough revenue and cash flow. Some support may be needed during the transition 
period before cash generation stabilizes.
•
EMIB-T / HBM5 / technology capability and competition
The entry barrier is the ability to integrate multiple technologies into manufacturable, 
scalable products: advanced packaging, HBM5, 448G SerDes, and low power. It is 
not any single technology.
•
Competitors are strong, and MediaTek does not assume it is far ahead. However, it is 
confident it will win its share of the business.
•
NVLink Fusion benefits and customer interest
It is a business model with strong technical content, and it makes it much easier for 
customers to integrate their own designs.
•
The key advantage is much faster time to compute power.•
MediaTek and nVidia are in discussions with several potential customers. Feedback 
is positive, and MediaTek hopes to announce cases in the near term.
•
448G
448G SerDes has already taped out.•
448G is a must going forward. It is an N6 design with very stringent power 
consumption requirements.
•
It will not be on the critical path, and MediaTek will improve interoperability to serve a 
broader customer base.
•

Definition of Nomura Group's equity research rating system and sectors
The rating system is a relative system, indicating expected performance against a specific benchmark identified for each individual stock, 
subject to limited management discretion. An analyst’s target price is an assessment of the current intrinsic fair value of the stock based on an 
appropriate valuation methodology determined by the analyst. Valuation methodologies include, but are not limited to, discounted cash flow 
analysis, expected return on equity and multiple analysis. Analysts may also indicate expected absolute upside/downside relative to the stated 
target price, defined as (target price - current price)/current price.

SECTORS
A 'Bullish' stance, indicates that the analyst expects the sector to outperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Neutral' stance, 
indicates that the analyst expects the sector to perform in line with the Benchmark during the next 12 months. A 'Bearish' stance, indicates that 
the analyst expects the sector to underperform the Benchmark during the next 12 months. Sectors that are labelled as 'Not rated' or shown as 
'N/A' are not assigned ratings. Benchmarks are as follows: United States: S&P 500; Europe: Dow Jones STOXX 600; Global Emerging 
Markets (ex-Asia): MSCI Emerging Markets ex-Asia. Japan/Asia ex-Japan: Sector ratings are not assigned.

Target Price
A Target Price, if discussed, indicates the analyst’s forecast for the share price with a 12-month time horizon, reflecting in part the analyst's 
estimates for the company's earnings. The achievement of any target price may be impeded by general market and macroeconomic trends, and 
by other risks related to the company or the market, and may not occur if the company's earnings differ from estimates.

### Page 1

Hon Hai Precision Industry

HHTD 2026: precision manufacturing, 
vertical integration, AI DC, and new EV

Maintain Rating: BUY | PO: 320.00 TWD | Price: 245.50 TWD

7th HHTD: precision manufacturing, vertical integration 
Hon Hai hosted its seventh Tech Day, titled "Accelerating Intelligence" on 30 Oct. Key 
highlights included its strengths in precision manufacturing, vertical integration, robotics 
and AI datacenters, as well as the launch of the Model A+ and Model K/K+ EVs. We 
expect Hon Hai, with its strong manufacturing capability and vertical integration 
advantages, to sustain its leading position as an AI server ODM. Maintain Buy. 
Doubled AI rack production to 2k racks per week currently  
Hon Hai's Chairman indicated that the company has shortened the assembly time for a 
compute tray from 2 hours to 20 minutes. This was supported by its precision 
manufacturing capability (1µm precision), automation and a cableless design co-
developed with its customer. Amid continued strong AI demand, Hon Hai has doubled its 
AI server rack production from 1,000 racks per week a year ago to 2,000 racks per week. 
AI server solution offerings across L1-L12 and CPO 
The company can now provide up to 50% of server rack components (excluding 
GPUs/CPUs), with solution offerings spanning L1 to L12. It demonstrated its high-speed 
cables/connectors, power busbars/whips, liquid cooling solutions (manifolds/cold 
plates/UQDs), compute/switch trays including the NVIDIA Spectrum-X CPO switch tray, 
server racks, as well as SuperPOD and AI datacenter solutions. 
Robotics for factory automation and new EV 
Hon Hai showcased live demonstrations of its robotics, highlighting capabilities such as 
AI-planned assembly paths and real-time sensing and tracking. For EVs, the company 
launched Model A+ and Model K/K+. Model A+ features L4 readiness, four-wheel drive 
and a spacious cabin. Model K converts from a truck into a van with a canopy box, while 
Model K+ features a more stylish design to serve the Japanese and European markets.

Company Sector 
Electronics Manufacturing Services

Company Description 
Hon Hai is the world's largest EMS provider, with four key 
business segments - Smart Consumer Electronics, Cloud & 
Networking, Computing, and Components & Others. Hon 
Hai supplies components, final assembly, and global 
logistics for global tech giants, including Apple, Microsoft, 
Google, HP, Dell, Cisco, and Sony. Hon Hai has 
R&D/production sites globally, including Taiwan, China, 
India, Japan, Vietnam, Malaysia, Czech Republic, Mexico, and 
the US. The company was established in Taiwan in 1974.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Hon Hai as its gradual moves into 
high margin/value-added server/components/semi 
business, stronger position in AI server with rising sales, 
solid balance sheet, and attractive valuation among Greater 
China AI server players.

### Page 6

Price objective basis & risk 
Hon Hai Precision Industry (HNHAF) 
We set our PO at NT$320 (15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS), at around 3SD above historical P/E 
average since 2007. We think the valuation is justified as HH's leading position in 
EMS/ODM business, improving iPhone momentum with replacement cycle, rising sales 
from AI business, more diversified global footprint than peers to mitigate 
tariff/geopolitical risk, and improving balance sheet with higher cash dividend payout.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) weaker momentum from Apple business (iPhone, iPad 
and Apple Watch), (2) increasing competition in EMS business from peers, (3) slower 
sales/earnings delivery in auto/EV business, and (4) weaker market demand or lower 
shipment from Nvidia AI server rack/system business.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) better-than-expected yield rate and demand for iPhone, 
iPad and Apple Watch business, (2) further automation adoption to reduce labor costs 
and improve efficiency, (3) faster sales/earnings delivery from auto/EV business, and (4) 
stronger market demand or higher shipment from Nvidia AI server rack/system business.

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation). 
Investment rating Total return expectation (within 12-month period of date of initial

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be

sector, region or other classification(s). A stock’ s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Wiwynn (6669.TW)

3
Wiwynn
Valuation
Our target price for Wiwynn is NT$3,200. We value Wiwynn based on PE as we believe the company’s earnings performance
and industry dynamics will be the key focus areas for the market. Our TP is based on 16x 2027E EPS, which is at the top end of
the historical trading range since listing and above the average PE multiple of the peer group. We believe our target multiple is
justified by the company’s 100% exposure to data centers, rising scale, and robust earnings growth, as well as stabilizing ROE.

Risks
Key downside risks that could cause Wiwynn’s shares to trade below our target price include: 1) Potential volatility in capex
deployment; 2) Component price hike; 3) Peer competition; and 4) FX volatility.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 Y oY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 Y oY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮 아 지
면서 전년 동기 수 익 성 악화에 크게 영 향 을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조 지 아 텔 루 라 이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스 포 티 지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수 요 가 높은 북미 현 지 에 서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중 심 의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추 정 치 는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부 정 적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것 으 로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상 반 기 적 용 한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목 표 주 가 를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상 대 적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모 멘 텀 에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일)

### Page 1

Despite a long list of risks, 2027 could deliver a surprisingly
benign global backdrop of resilient growth, easing inﬂation and
steady central banks. But political disruption in Europe and
mounting ﬁscal pressures could quickly challenge that outlook
Carsten Brzeski
A brighter outlook for the global economy
ING's base call: All will be good
The list of potential downside risks is long, but the global
economy will enjoy another year of resilience. In the US, the

Trump administration is riding into the sunset without
disruptive ﬁreworks, sparing the global economy new trade
tensions and new wars.
Instead, the investment boom in AI might slow down
somewhat, but infrastructure investments and AI adoption
will support growth in many economies as energy prices
return to more normal levels. At the same time, the current

### Page 2

inﬂation wave will lose momentum, allowing central banks to
keep their powder dry.
The mix of modest growth, slowing inﬂation and policy rates
being only mildly restrictive could surprise many.
Admittedly, this is a scenario accompanied by a lot of
uncertainty and noise, but underneath this well-known layer
of risk and fear, an almost benign macro environment could
emerge in 2027. What a nice change.
Our risky call: Transatlantic divide makes an
unexpected turn

Over the last few years, Europe has often been left ﬂabbergasted
by political developments in the US and the increasingly hostile
transatlantic relationship. The US midterm results may now raise
hopes that those ties can gradually recover.
Yet just as the US appears to be stepping back from populist and
hegemonic policies, Europe could be moving in the opposite
direction. With elections next year in France, Italy, Poland, Spain,
and maybe even Germany, Europe risks ushering in a new period of

political disruption, with stronger eurosceptic forces weighing on
sentiment and investment and complicating further European
integration.
One upside of Donald Trump’s second term in oﬃce was that his
politics and policies created a common adversary and brought
Europe closer together. The irony of 2027 could be that, just as
Trump enters his lame-duck period, political divisions within Europe
begin to do the damage to European unity that external pressure

from Washington never could.
Our bold call: Global ﬁscal dominance
Our bold call for the global economy sees many governments
struggling with the pressures of higher interest rates, rising debt
levels and the need to increase investments, while ensuring social
or societal peace.

under control, with the option to still write oﬀ government bonds
once they are on central banks’ balance sheets.

Document 322: Anthropic加速推进IPO，计划联手摩根大通CEO直播推介

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic加速推进IPO，计划联手摩根大通CEO直播推介.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-10
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 计划于本周五安排 CEO 达⾥奥与摩根⼤通 CEO 戴蒙进⾏直播推介，周⼆已启动
路演，冲刺感恩节前登录纳斯达克。公司拟参考 SpaceX 模式筹资超 800 亿美元，由⾼盛领
衔主承销，摩根⼤通负责散⼾发售。近期市场估值预期已⾃近 2 万亿降⾄ 1.1 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿
美元。
摘要：
Anthropic 正加快推进潜在的创纪录 IPO 。为给上市造势，公司计划安排联合创始⼈兼 CEO 达⾥
奥 · 阿莫迪（ Dario Amodei ）与摩根⼤通董事⻓兼 CEO 杰⽶ · 戴蒙（ Jamie Dimon ）进⾏⼀场直
播对谈，向潜在投资者推介公司及其 AI 业务前景。
据彭博援引知情⼈⼠报道，这场直播预计将在本周五纽约午餐时间前后举⾏。 Anthropic 本周⼆
启动正式路演，并争取在美国感恩节假期前完成定价、登陆纳斯达克。
不过，在近期 AI 板块估值回调的背景下， Anthropic 的定价预期已明显降温。市场⼈⼠⽬前讨论
的估值区间约为1.1 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿美元，较此前⼀度接近 2 万亿美元的预期⼤幅回落。

直播推介参考 SpaceX 模式
知情⼈⼠称，阿莫迪与戴蒙的直播对谈，在形式上参考了 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉ IPO 期间安排⻢斯克出
席推介活动的做法，但整体规格将相对低调。
SpaceX 当时通过 IPO 及超额配售合计募资 862 亿美元，创下美股 IPO 募资纪录。 Anthropic 此次
计划募集的资⾦规模据称可能与之相当，甚⾄更⾼。
对于相关安排， Anthropic 和摩根⼤通均拒绝置评。
此次由戴蒙参与直播，也凸显了摩根⼤通与 Anthropic 之间⽇益密切的合作关系。本周⼀，
Anthropic 曾在官⽹披露，摩根⼤通正将旗下编程⼯具 Claude Code 从软件⼯程团队推⼴⾄更多
业务部⻔。
⾼盛主承销，摩根⼤通主攻散⼾发⾏
从承销架构看，⾼盛获得了最关键的左侧主承销商席位，摩根⼠丹利则担任稳定代理商，相关
信息已体现在 Anthropic 提交给美国证券交易委员会的⽂件中。
摩根⼤通是参与此次交易的 22 家银⾏之⼀，主要负责美国散⼾投资者的营销和股份配售⼯作。

此前有报道称， Anthropic 正考虑将约 15% ⾄ 20% 的 IPO 股份分配给散⼾投资者。
花旗集团则负责协调国际零售发⾏，覆盖英国、欧洲部分地区、加拿⼤、⽇本和澳⼤利亚等市
场。
在⼤型科技公司 IPO 中，提⾼散⼾配售⽐例有助于扩⼤市场参与度和交易热度，但也会对上市后
的股价表现带来更⾼要求。尤其是在当前市场波动加剧的背景下， Anthropic 能否平衡融资规
模、估值⽔平与⼆级市场表现，成为投资者关注的重点。
AI 估值回调，机构要求 “ 留出安全边际 ”

Anthropic 的上市定价正⾯临来⾃市场的压⼒。
据 华 尔 街 ⻅ 闻 11 ⽉ 6 ⽇ ⽂ 章 ， 在 纳 斯 达 克 100 指 数 单 ⽇ 下 跌 6.4% 后 ， 部 分 潜 在 投 资 者 将
Anthropic 的合理估值区间下调⾄ 1.2 万亿⾄ 1.3 万亿美元。相⽐之下，在 10 ⽉公司财务简报后，
投资者最初给出的反馈约为 1.4 万亿⾄ 1.5 万亿美元；⽽在 10 ⽉初，市场对其估值的最⾼预期⼀度
接近 2 万亿美元。⽬前，相关讨论仍在进⾏，最终定价尚未确定。
多位机构⼈⼠认为， Anthropic 需要在估值上作出⾜够让步，才能吸引⻓期资⾦并保证上市后的
交易表现。
Gallo Partners ⾸席投资官 Michael Alfaro 表⽰， Anthropic 若想建⽴ “ ⾼质量的订单簿 ” ，估值应控

制在 1.3 万亿⾄ 1.4 万亿美元之间。 Sycomore Asset Management 科技组合经理 David Rainville
则认为，若定价接近 1.2 万亿美元，吸引投资者 “ 不会有太⼤困难 ” 。
汇丰⾸席多资产策略师 Max Kettner 的态度更为谨慎。他认为， Anthropic 的估值应低于 1 万亿美
元，原因并不只是发⾏能否完成，更在于上市后的股价能否站稳。
“ 更重要的是⼆级市场表现， ”Kettner 表⽰， “ 如果出现类似 SpaceX 的情况 —— 投资者在⼆级市场
买⼊后，股价短暂冲⾼、随后迅速回落 —— 这会是当前市场环境中最不希望看到的结果。 ”
对 Anthropic ⽽⾔，这场由阿莫迪和戴蒙参与的直播，或许能够强化公司在技术实⼒、商业化进
展和⾦融机构合作上的叙事；但最终决定 IPO 成败的，仍将是估值是否⾜够贴近市场的⻛险偏
好。
限免

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -2.62 điểm tương ứng -0.15%, với thanh khoản cải 
thiện. Dệt may, Công nghệ và Máy móc là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất trong 
tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -13,987 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã HDB, PNJ và VJC; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-30 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại đảo chiều mua ròng 1,267 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần 
qua nhưng đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -2,363 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 3,726 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -2,702 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam trải qua một tuần đi ngang giằng co, giảm nhẹ và đóng cửa ở 1.735 điểm 
với thanh khoản ở mức thấp, và nhà đầu tư nước ngoài bán ròng cả 5 phiên trong tuần. 
Thông tin vĩ mô tích cực với tăng trưởng GDP quý 3 đạt mức gần 10% là điểm sáng trong 
tuần qua, tuy đã không đủ nâng đỡ thị trường chung. Thay vào đó, tâm lý thị trường thận 
trọng trước số liệu lạm phát ở mức cao, đợt tăng giá xăng dầu trong nước và mặt bằng lãi 
suất trong nước chưa có dấu hiệu hạ nhiệt. Diễn biến tăng của giá dầu thế giới, và lợi suất 
trái phiếu Chính phủ Mỹ cũng là các yếu tố gây áp lực lên thị trường chung. Tâm lý thận 
trọng còn thể hiện qua áp lực bán giải chấp ở một số cổ phiếu riêng lẻ như PNJ, SHB..., dù 
một vài nhóm trụ như dầu khí, tiêu dùng có thời điểm đóng vai trò nâng đỡ chỉ số.

Trần Đức Anh 
Giám đốc Kinh tế Vĩ mô & Chiến lược thị trường 
anhtd@kbsec.com.vn 
07/10/2026

Triển vọng TTCK 4Q2026 
Tích lũy trong vùng nhiễu động

Chúng tôi dự báo vùng điểm hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm 2026 
xuống 1,800 đi ểm, giảm mạnh từ mức 1,950 đi ểm đưa ra trong báo cáo gần nhất. 
Vùng điểm này tương ứng:

- Tăng trưởng EPS 2026 so với cùng kỳ của thị trường chung ở mức 33% 
(tăng mạnh so với mức 25% của báo cáo gần nhất), chủ yếu nhờ kỳ vọng 
vào các chính sách thúc đẩy kinh tế của Chính phủ, và lợi nhuận tăng 
trưởng đột biến ở VIC (xem thêm phần Triển vọng hoạt động kinh doanh).

- Mức P/E hợp lý của chỉ số VNIndex thời điểm cuối năm ở mức 13.2 (giảm 
nhẹ so với mức 13.5 thời điểm hiện tại - phản ánh quan điểm các yếu tố 
rủi ro sẽ tiếp tục duy trì). Đây là mức định giá thấp so với bình quân 5 năm 
gần nhất ở 15.1. Nếu loại bỏ yếu tố đột biến ở nhóm Vingroup, chỉ số P/E 
điều chỉnh hiện đã rơi xuống 10.9, thấp hơn các đáy gần nhất năm 2020, 
2022 và 2025. Trong bối cảnh kinh tế Việt Nam duy trì tốc độ tăng trưởng 
cao, lợi nhuận các doanh nghiệp niêm yết liên tục bứt phá, vùng định giá 
này mở ra cơ hội đầu tư trung/dài hạn. Chúng tôi cho rằng ngay khi các rủi 
ro vĩ mô quốc tế (xoay quanh vấn đề Iran), và trong nước (xoay quanh vấn 
đề lãi suất) hạ nhiệt, thị trường chung sẽ có cơ hội tái định giá lên mức cao 
hơn đáng kể so với thời điểm hiện tại. Dù vậy, khác với báo cáo gần nhất 
vào tháng 7, ở báo cáo này, chúng tôi đẩy lui thời điểm các yếu tố rủi ro vĩ 
mô hạ nhiệt sang năm 2027, đồng nghĩa với quan điểm thận trọng hơn về 
thị trường cho quý 4 2026.

Cụ thể, đối với vấn đề lãi suất, áp lực lạm phát lớn dần, đi kèm nhu cầu vốn cao phục 
vụ hoạt động đầu tư cơ sở hạ tầng sẽ khiến mặt bằng lãi suất duy trì ở mức cao cho 
phần còn lại của năm 2026 (dù thanh khoản hệ thống ghi nhận sự cải thiện đáng kể 
trong 1 quý gần đây). Đối với vấn đề xung đột Trung Đông, các cuộc đàm phán vẫn 
đang bế tắc và ít có kh ả năng dòng ch ảy dầu mỏ tại khu vực này được nối lại ngay 
trong năm nay.

Chủ đề đầu tư chúng tôi l ựa chọn cho 4Q2026 là đ ầu tư công, chính sách ti ền tệ hỗ 
trợ, và xung đột Iran.

Xem đầy đủ tại đây.

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX hình thành mẫu nến Doji trên khung đồ thị tuần sau hai tuần giảm điểm liên tiếp, 
cho thấy áp lực bán đã phần nào được kiểm soát. Trên khung ngày, nỗ lực hồi phục đầu 
tuần gặp lực cản tại đường SMA50 khiến VNINDEX quay đầu giảm điểm, tiếp tục chớm phá 
đáy ngắn hạn; tuy nhiên vùng hỗ trợ gần đã tạo hiệu ứng thu hút lực cầu bắt đáy, giúp chỉ 
số thu hẹp đà giảm trong phiên cuối tuần. Sau nhịp điều chỉnh khá tiêu cực từ vùng đỉnh 
đầu tháng 9, VNINDEX đang bước vào giai đoạn tìm kiếm điểm cân bằng trở lại và diễn biến 
giằng co điều chỉnh có thể còn kéo dài thêm trong các phiên kế tiếp. Tuy nhiên khi xu 
hướng tăng ngắn hạn tính từ vùng đáy cuối tháng 7 vẫn đang được bảo lưu, chỉ số được kỳ 
vọng sẽ có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại tại điểm đỡ gần quanh 1710 (đây cũng là chốt chặn cần 
giữ để bảo vệ được xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn). 
Kháng cự xa: 1820 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1775 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1710 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1670 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 368: Democratic victories to constrain President Trump and increase tech scrutiny

Source PDF: all report pdf/Democratic victories to constrain President Trump and increase tech scrutiny.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-04

### Page 1

The opinion polls called it right with sweeping victories for the
Democrats in the midterm elections. The key question is
whether the president will look to secure deals or whether
gridlock on the domestic front will force him to look outwards

James Knightley, Carsten Brzeski, Padhraic Garvey, CFA, Lynn
Song and Chris Turner
Rep. Katherine Clark, the Democratic House minority whip, House Democratic
Leader Hakeem Jeﬀries, and Democratic Congressional Campaign Committee
Chair Suzan DelBene
The results are in and it's a big Democratic
victory
With most votes counted, it's clear the Democrats have secured a
decisive victory in the US midterms, claiming majorities in both the
Senate and House of Representatives. Though this outcome had
become the consensus in recent weeks, the scale of the Democratic
victory in the Senate in particular is noteworthy. Wins in Texas, but
also in less expected states like South Carolina, Kansas and Iowa,

Dealmaker-in-Chief or Mr Veto?
The Republican majorities in the House and Senate in the ﬁrst half
of Trump's presidential term allowed him to sign the One Big
Beautiful Bill Act into law in July 2025. Other legislation included
the Laken Riley Act, which tightened immigration and border policy,
the GENIUS Act, which created a regulatory structure for

stablecoins, the HALT Fentanyl Act on drugs and the TAKE IT DOWN
Act on online regulation.

### Page 3

The obvious outcome from the result on Tuesday is that a
Democrat-controlled Congress will stiﬂe President Trump’s domestic
agenda in his ﬁnal two years in power. A bill has to be approved by
both the House and the Senate before being passed on to the
president to sign or veto. With the Democrats having control of the
legislative agenda in the House and Senate, the president has
already warned that he will likely become “Mr Veto”. The only way
to overcome that would be for the Democrats to secure a two-thirds

supermajority in both houses. That isn't the case after last night's
results.
The consequence is that spending and tax proposals, as with most
domestic legislation, will likely be gridlocked. There are potential
areas of compromise tied to healthcare aﬀordability in exchange
for perhaps more spending on defence, but major tax and spending
changes appear unlikely.
On the one hand, this is often viewed positively, as it provides a
stable backdrop for corporate America. On the other, it means there

is little prospect of meaningful change to the nation’s ﬁscal stance.
While we won’t see any further ﬁscal expansion, large ﬁscal deﬁcits
and deteriorating debt dynamics will continue.
The ﬁscal situation will certainly remain a concern, although hitting
the debt ceiling could act as a temporary brake. The issue is likely
to come to a head by mid-summer 2027, with little scope for
compromise between the two sides in Congress. This risks an
extended government shutdown, potentially exceeding last

October’s shutdown, which depressed GDP growth in 4Q 2025 by a
full percentage point. The Federal Reserve risks “ﬂying blind” again
with no government economic statistics being collated or released.
What the Democrats can seek to achieve
Key sectors of corporate America will be nervous that Democratic
control of important oversight committees could bring greater
scrutiny. More congressional hearings involving AI and technology
business leaders would be a likely outcome, while a strong

performance at the ballot box may embolden local Democratic

### Page 4

leaders to try to stall unpopular data centre construction in their
constituencies. Fossil fuel producers may face similar challenges.
Democrats will likely feel even more conﬁdent about pushing back
against President Trump’s policies on Iran, support of Israel and any
escalation of tariﬀs. They may apply more pressure on the
president to sound tougher towards Russia and to provide support
to Ukraine, but the ﬁnal call will remain with the president.

Investigations into President Trump, his family and members of his
administration may also be stepped up as Democrats exercise their
greater powers. Inquiries into ﬁnances and business dealings could
result in subpoenas for documents and testimony. Moreover,
impeachment proceedings begin in the House, raising the
possibility of a third attempt to impeach the president.
To be convicted and removed from oﬃce, 67 of the 100 senators
would need to vote in favour, which is unlikely. The Democrats could

still use the process to constrain the president, but doing so risks
further toxifying the political situation in the US. Rather than
pursuing such action against the president himself, Democrats
could instead launch investigations into members of his cabinet.
With Democrats now in control of the Senate, they can block or, at
the very least, stall judicial and executive nominees. This includes
heads of government agencies, ambassadors and cabinet

members, potentially making it diﬃcult for the president to replace
any senior oﬃcials he chooses to ﬁre.
What will the president do?
A key question is whether a constrained President Trump, entering
the ﬁnal phase of his tenure, becomes more willing to accept
political or market disruption in pursuit of his objectives.
If there is little sense of any compromise, the ensuing gridlock in
Congress would leave the president to focus on areas where he can

exercise executive power: 1) foreign policy and diplomacy, including
trade policy, 2) national security and defence, 3) law enforcement
and regulatory policy, 4) personnel appointments (subject to
Senate approval), and 5) administration and management policy.

### Page 5

This suggests Iran, Russia/Ukraine and tariﬀs/trade policy are likely
to be the focus for the president. Tariﬀ policy could increasingly be
used to incentivise foreign partners to align with US foreign policy
objectives, for example by pressuring NATO members to increase
defence spending.
More fundamentally, heavy defeats in the House and Senate will be
interpreted as a referendum on President Trump’s policies and
potentially prompt ﬁgures within the Republican Party to take the

view that it is time to open the debate on its future direction. With
Trump’s inﬂuence set to fade, leading lights within the party, most
notably JD Vance and Marco Rubio, may attempt to shore up
support for their own campaigns as they look to the 2028
presidential election. Similarly, a large Democratic victory will
intensify speculation over whether the party’s 2028 candidate
should come from its more moderate wing or whether the
progressive left has enough momentum to put forward a candidate

of its own.
The market reaction
The initial bond market reaction has been a fall in yields across the
curve. In the coming days, that could reverse as the prospect of
policy stasis leaves the market weighing a still-elevated ﬁscal
deﬁcit against some easing of inﬂation concerns. We will probably
see front-end yields drift lower, while the back end retains an upside
bias. If we do see more tariﬀs, the back end could ﬁnd a small silver
lining in the additional tax revenues. But any beneﬁt is likely to be

marginal, given the risk that tariﬀs also push inﬂation higher.
Overall, we retain a bias towards the 10-year yield heading towards
5.25%.
The dollar is softer on the outcome, though it has recovered from its
lows seen in early Asia when a sharp fall in Nasdaq futures was
warning of a larger re-pricing of the tech story. Nasdaq futures have
since retraced the majority of those losses. Limiting a larger dollar
correction on this news is probably a still-resilient US economy and

at least one more rate hike from the Federal Reserve. The dollar
could see a little more weakness into 2027, however, if an

### Page 6

emboldened Democratic Party takes greater issue with Republican
policy, including deregulation and the AI build-out. And the
potential for a prolonged government shutdown in mid-2027 adds
to the case for a weaker dollar next summer.
What it means for Europe and China
With the outcome of the vote limiting President Trump’s options at
home, he is likely to focus more heavily on foreign and trade policy.
The result could be greater volatility and uncertainty for the global

economy.
This will clearly aﬀect Europe. A forced deal between Russia and
Ukraine, to the disadvantage of Ukraine and Europe, could
undermine economic sentiment in Europe. A new escalation in the
Middle East would push up energy prices and harm the European
economy. Europe’s latest shift towards more protectionist trade
policies could bring new global trade tensions.
At the same time, with only two years left in oﬃce, the TACO
(Trump Always Chickens Out) phenomenon could increasingly

shape Europe’s thinking. The risk of an unwarranted economic
accident rises as Trump’s policies towards Europe become more
aggressive and Europe’s own stance hardens, in the hope that
Trump will ultimately back down or simply run out of time.
If Republicans lay the blame for the poor result at the president’s
door, the sense that Trump’s time is coming to an end could grow
stronger. If so, some European countries may be tempted to test
the president’s patience and slow the pace of defence spending

increases, particularly given their own ﬁscal constraints.
Alternatively, and less likely, a stiﬂed but less angry Trump
administration could try to bring the wars in Iran and Ukraine to an
end. Constrained by Congress and the Supreme Court on trade, it
could ultimately, intentionally or otherwise, end up indirectly
supporting the European economy.
China’s main response will be to wait and see, as it will react to
actual policy changes rather than the shifts within Congress. The

Personal ties between the two leaders appear good, but with
President Trump’s policy focus being channelled towards foreign
and trade policy, China could end up in the crosshairs again. The
trade truce is set to expire in January, and it seems like both sides

Europe, if Trump appears weakened by the outcome of the
midterms, China could sense that it is time to start looking beyond

2028, triggering a revaluation of its long-term US strategy.

Document 376: Euro Credit Supply_ October slowdown

Source PDF: all report pdf/Euro Credit Supply_ October slowdown.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 7

### Page 1

Euro Credit Supply November 2026

1 
- 9bn 127   
Euro Credit Supply  
Supply slowdown in October

Corporate issuance slows in October, net supply remains positive 
• Euro corporate issuance totalled €28bn in October, down from €56bn in September. 
Despite the softer monthly volume, YTD issuance reached €426bn, ahead of the 
€413bn YTD in 2020 and remaining above the €401bn issued over the same period 
in 2025. Corporate net supply remained positive at €2.2bn in October, bringing the 
YTD total to €154bn.  
• TMT remained the largest contributor to euro corporate issuance, with €8bn printed 
in October and €110bn issued year-to-date, up 30% versus last year. Utilities have 
also remained an active source of supply, with YTD issuance reaching €74bn (+28% 
YoY), while Industrials & Chemicals issuance totalled €59bn year-to-date. 
• Corporate Reverse Yankee issuance amounted to €7bn in October, lifting YTD 
volume to €104bn. This has already exceeded the €92bn issued during the whole of 
2025, making 2026 the largest year on record for corporate Reverse Yankee 
issuance.  
• Corporate ESG issuance reached €95bn year-to-date, exceeding the €76bn recorded 
over the same period in 2025. Green bonds continued to account for the majority of 
issuance, with YTD volumes reaching €87bn.  
Bank bond supply drops in October with net supply in negative territory 
• EUR financial supply totalled €17.5bn in October, down from the €49bn high 
recorded in September. This brings 2026 YTD gross issuance to €338bn and YTD net 
issuance to just below €111bn. October net supply was negative for the second time 
this year at nearly €-5bn, due to redemptions totalling around €22bn last month.  
• Covered bond issuances reached €8.5bn in October, a €16bn decline from 
September's level. This was the second-lowest monthly issuance volume recorded 
since the start of 2026, on a backdrop of spread widening at the beginning of the 
month. YTD covered bond supply is reaching €180bn, still nearly €30bn ahead of last 
year's level, despite last month's slowdown. 
• Bank senior unsecured issuance totalled just over €12bn in October, of which €8.4bn 
in senior non-preferred bonds and €3.8bn in senior preferred instruments. YTD 
senior unsecured supply stands at €214bn. While senior preferred bond supply is at 
a similar level to 2025 YTD, senior non-preferred bond issuance is running nearly 
€10bn above last year's level.  
• Sustainable bond supply slowed to €2.3bn in October, down over €7bn MoM. YTD 
ESG issuances have reached over €67bn, the highest level recorded since 2022. The 
increase was driven by green bonds (reaching €60.7bn versus €41.8bn in 2025 YTD). 
Social bond supply declined to €4.5bn from €6.8bn the year prior. Sustainable 
issuances remain broadly stable at €2bn. 
Timothy Rahill 
Credit Strategist 
timothy.rahill@ing.com 
Marine Leleux 
Sector Strategist, Financials 
marine.leleux2@ing.com

Document 442: Robust US jobs market boosts December rate hike chances

Source PDF: all report pdf/Robust US jobs market boosts December rate hike chances.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

The Federal Reserve has been focusing more on inﬂation
recently, but a strong jobs report for October makes
a December rate hike an even easier decision
James Knightley
US non-farm payrolls in October were above expectations with manufacturing
showing signs of life
165,000 Jobs added in October
Jobs up, unemployment down, wages
growth holding up
The October US jobs report looks very decent. Non-farm payrolls
rose 165k versus the 75k consensus forecast and there were 84k of

upward revisions to the past two months’ data. The unemployment
rate dropped to 4.1% from 4.2% and wage growth rose
0.4%MoM/3.1%YoY versus the consensus prediction of 0.3%/2.9%.

### Page 2

That certainly helps the case for a December Federal Reserve
interest rate hike, expectations for which had slipped in recent days
to make it close to a 50:50 call at one point. We are now back to
20bp priced for the December FOMC meeting, and we fully expect
the Fed to deliver at that meeting.
The details show that, yet again, it is private education and
healthcare services leading the gains (+53k), but manufacturing is
showing real life with a 19k increase as it becomes increasingly

competitive to produce certain products in the US. Secure and
relatively low-priced energy, a strong economy, huge tech
investment spending and, at the margin, tariﬀs, have all boosted
the sector. Manufacturing has now added 86k jobs over the past
ﬁve months after three consecutive years of decline. Other sectors
that performed well include temporary help (+19k) and transport
and warehousing (+25k), but leisure and hospitality sank 13k with
construction employment falling 3k.

long pause

The Federal Reserve is focusing more on the inﬂation aspect of its
mandate right now, but a stronger jobs story remains important for

the rate hike narrative. While we do expect a December rate rise, we
continue to doubt the need for additional hikes next year. The
market has moved in that direction in recent days so that it no
longer fully prices additional hikes in 2027, presumably because of
the big drop in energy prices and diminished fears of second-round
price eﬀects. Next week's CPI report is expected to show core CPI
rising by 0.2% month-on-month, which should support that story.

### Page 3

Global Market Outlook 3

12m Foundation Overweights: 
• Gold 
• US, Asia ex-Japan equities 
• Emerging Market USD bonds 
Opportunistic Ideas – Equities: 
• Global: High dividend 
• US: Healthcare^ 
• Euro area (EA): Banks, MSCI 
Switzerland^ 
• Asia: Japan banks 
Top Global Sectors: 
• US: Technology, healthcare^, 
financials, materials 
• EA: Financials 
• Asia: Japan financials 
Opportunistic Ideas – Bonds: 
• US: Treasury Inflation-protected 
Securities (TIPS), AAA-rated 
collateralised loan obligations 
(CLOs), US High-yield (HY) 
• Others: AUD corporates  
^New 
Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds . Near-
term event risks and high  earnings expectations prompt us to trim  equities to a 
Core holding.  The US and Asia ex -Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive  income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain  Overweight gold . Prices are likely to grind higher as near -term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high  earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead.  In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran . These  concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly f or the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite t he current c hallenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop . The US economic outlook remains well supported , and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

1 Investment strategy and key themes

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer 
Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA 
Raymond Cheng, CFA, CPA 
Chief Investment Officer, North Asia

### Page 4

Global Market Outlook 4

Fig. 1 Our quant model score has fallen to levels 
associated with flat-to-modestly-negative equity returns  
 Fig. 2 Negative gold-USD correlation has held back 
gold amid USD strength. This is unlikely to persist 
Our quantitative stock-bond model  Gold and USD correlations over the last 15 years

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered  Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long -term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects  this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar  by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and A xJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect the m to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers.  Our positive view on both markets’  
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India.  Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI -related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification . In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic idea s also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months  – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment -grade (IG) 
and high -yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3 -7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance.  We close our EU  bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29 ) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective  tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream , while  rotating into alternatives  
helps reduce  equity beta exposure . We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub -
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4 ,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

Global Market Outlook 5

The Foundation and Foundation+ models are allocations that you can use as the starting point for building a diversified 
investment portfolio. The Foundation model showcases a set of allocations focusing on traditional asset classes that are 
accessible to most investors, while the Found ation+ model includes allocations to private assets that may be accessible to 
investors in some jurisdictions, but not others.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk.  While we remain constructive on the long -term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near -term risk -reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation  and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long -term AI growth opportunities.  The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI -related beneficiaries. For investors, t he 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk , and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem.  
Non-AI-related factors , including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk . This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50%  to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses , although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI -related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversific ation. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long -term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives,  and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability):  We maintain the probability at 30% a s AI-driven investment growth  continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027 , as AI-led expansion continues.  The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4 %, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2. 3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026 , but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risk s 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures ( PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees c ore PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2. 4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates  at 4.0% , after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.
